,

\displaystyle[u_{l},v_{l}](\mathcal{I}\oplus_{0}\mathcal{J})=\begin{cases}\text{interval $\mathcal{J}_{l}$ steps after $[u_{l-1},v_{l-1}](\mathcal{I}\oplus_{0}\mathcal{J})$}\quad&\text{if}\ \mathcal{I}_{l}=0,\\
[u_{|\mathcal{I}_{1:l}|},v_{|\mathcal{I}_{1:l}|}]\quad&\text{if}\ \mathcal{I}_{l}=1,\\
\end{cases}

where
|\mathcal{I}_{1:l}|=\mathcal{I}_{1}+\ldots+\mathcal{I}_{l}
, and write

\displaystyle S^{k^{\prime}}(\mathbb{X}^{\rho})_{[0,T]}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{k^{\prime}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{1},\ldots,i_{j}\geq 1\\
i_{1}+\cdots+i_{j}=k^{\prime}\end{subarray}}\sum_{j^{\prime}=1}^{j}\sum_{\mathcal{I}\oplus_{0}\mathcal{J}\in\mathcal{S}_{j^{\prime},j,m}}\underset{[u_{1},v_{1}]<_{m_{1}}\dots<_{m_{j^{\prime}-1}}[u_{j^{\prime}},v_{j^{\prime}}]\in\rho}{\sum\ \cdots\ \sum}\left[\prod_{l=1}^{j}\frac{1}{i_{l}!}\right]\bigotimes_{x=1}^{k^{\prime}}\mathbf{X}_{[u_{l_{x}},v_{l_{x}}](\mathcal{I}\oplus_{0}\mathcal{J})},

where, for each
l^{\prime}=1,\ldots,j^{\prime}-1
, we have
[u_{l^{\prime}},v_{l^{\prime}}]<_{m_{l^{\prime}}}[u_{l^{\prime}+1},v_{l^{\prime}+1}]
, i.e. there are at least
m_{l^{\prime}}
intervals between
[u_{l^{\prime}},v_{l^{\prime}}]
and
[u_{l^{\prime}+1},v_{l^{\prime}+1}]
in
\rho
, where

m_{l^{\prime}}=m_{l^{\prime}}(\mathcal{I}\oplus_{0}\mathcal{J})=\sum_{l=1}^{j}\mathcal{J}_{l}\,\mathds{1}_{\{|\mathcal{I}_{1:l}|=l^{\prime},\,\mathcal{I}_{l}=0\}}.

The structure of the tensor products in the summation is thus

\mathbf{X}^{\otimes i_{1}}_{u_{1},v_{1}}\otimes\mathbf{X}^{\otimes i_{2}}_{u^{2}_{1},v^{2}_{1}}\otimes\cdots\otimes\mathbf{X}^{\otimes i_{n_{1}}}_{u^{n_{1}}_{1},v^{n_{1}}_{1}}\otimes\mathbf{X}^{\otimes i_{n_{1}+1}}_{u_{2},v_{2}}\otimes\mathbf{X}^{\otimes i_{n_{1}+2}}_{u^{2}_{2},v^{2}_{2}}\otimes\cdots\otimes\mathbf{X}^{\otimes i_{n_{1}+n_{2}}}_{u^{n_{2}}_{2},v^{n_{2}}_{2}}\otimes\cdots,

where
[u_{1},v_{1}],[u^{2}_{1},v^{2}_{1}],\cdots,[u^{n_{1}}_{1},v^{n_{1}}_{1}]
are all less than
m
intervals apart and
[u_{2},v_{2}]
is at least
m
intervals after
[u^{n_{1}}_{1},v^{n_{1}}_{1}]
(and so on)
18
18

18

To help intuitive understanding consider the case where
k^{\prime}=2
and
m=1
, then the above expression reduces to

\displaystyle S^{2}(\mathbb{X}^{\rho})_{[0,T]}

\displaystyle=\sum_{[u_{1},v_{1}]\in\rho}\frac{\mathbf{X}_{u_{1},v_{1}}^{\otimes 2}}{2!}+\sum_{[u_{1},v_{1}]\in\rho}\mathbf{X}_{u_{1},v_{1}}\otimes\mathbf{X}_{v_{1},w_{1}}+\sum_{[u_{1},v_{1}]\in\rho}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}[u_{2},v_{2}]\in\rho\\
[u_{1},v_{1}]<_{1}[u_{2},v_{2}]\end{subarray}}\mathbf{X}_{u_{1},v_{1}}\otimes\mathbf{X}_{u_{2},v_{2}},

where
[v_{1},w_{1}]
is the interval right-contiguous to
[u_{1},v_{1}]
in
\rho
. If
k^{\prime}=2
and
m=2
, instead

\displaystyle S^{2}(\mathbb{X}^{\rho})_{[0,T]}

\displaystyle=\sum_{[u_{1},v_{1}]\in\rho}\frac{\mathbf{X}_{u_{1},v_{1}}^{\otimes 2}}{2!}+\sum_{[u_{1},v_{1}]\in\rho}\mathbf{X}_{u_{1},v_{1}}\otimes\mathbf{X}_{v_{1},w_{1}}+\sum_{[u_{1},v_{1}]\in\rho}\mathbf{X}_{u_{1},v_{1}}\otimes\mathbf{X}_{w_{1},z_{1}}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad+\sum_{[u_{1},v_{1}]\in\rho}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}[u_{2},v_{2}]\in\rho\\
[u_{1},v_{1}]<_{2}[u_{2},v_{2}]\end{subarray}}\mathbf{X}_{u_{1},v_{1}}\otimes\mathbf{X}_{u_{2},v_{2}},

where
[u_{1},v_{1}],[v_{1},w_{1}],[w_{1},z_{1}]
are consecutive intervals in
\rho
.
.

Let
n\geq 3
and define
\rho_{n}=(n-1)T+\rho
. Then, for each word
I=(w_{1},\ldots,w_{k^{\prime}})
of length
k^{\prime}
, we can write

\displaystyle\mathrm{Cov}\left(S^{I}(\mathbb{X}^{\rho,1})_{[0,T]},S^{I}(\mathbb{X}^{\rho,n})_{[0,T]}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j_{1},j_{2}=1}^{k^{\prime}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{1},\ldots,i_{j_{1}}\geq 1\\
i_{1}+\cdots+i_{j_{1}}=k^{\prime}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}e_{1},\ldots,e_{j_{2}}\geq 1\\
e_{1}+\cdots+e_{j_{2}}=k^{\prime}\end{subarray}}\sum_{j^{\prime}_{1}=1}^{j_{1}}\sum_{j^{\prime}_{2}=1}^{j_{2}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathcal{I}_{1}\oplus_{0}\mathcal{J}_{1}\in S_{j^{\prime}_{1},j_{1},m}\\
\mathcal{I}_{2}\oplus_{0}\mathcal{J}_{2}\in S_{j^{\prime}_{2},j_{2},m}\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}[u_{1},v_{1}]<_{m^{1}_{1}}\dots<_{m^{1}_{j^{\prime}_{1}-1}}[u_{j^{\prime}_{1}},v_{j^{\prime}_{1}}]\in\rho\\
[s_{1},t_{1}]<_{m^{2}_{1}}\dots<_{m^{2}_{j^{\prime}_{2}-1}}[s_{j^{\prime}_{2}},t_{j^{\prime}_{2}}]\in\rho_{n}\end{subarray}}{\sum\ \cdots\ \sum}\bigg[\prod_{l=1}^{j_{1}}\frac{1}{i_{l}!}\bigg]\bigg[\prod_{l=1}^{j_{2}}\frac{1}{e_{l}!}\bigg]\mathrm{Cov}\Bigg(\prod_{x=1}^{k^{\prime}}X^{(w_{x})}_{[u_{l^{1}_{x}},v_{l^{1}_{x}}](\mathcal{I}_{1}\oplus_{0}\mathcal{J}_{1})},\prod_{x=1}^{k^{\prime}}X^{(w_{x})}_{[s_{l^{2}_{x}},t_{l^{2}_{x}}](\mathcal{I}_{2}\oplus_{0}\mathcal{J}_{2})}\Bigg)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j_{1},j_{2}=1}^{k^{\prime}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{1},\ldots,i_{j_{1}}\geq 1\\
i_{1}+\cdots+i_{j_{1}}=k^{\prime}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}e_{1},\ldots,e_{j_{2}}\geq 1\\
e_{1}+\cdots+e_{j_{2}}=k^{\prime}\end{subarray}}\sum_{j^{\prime}_{1}=1}^{j_{1}}\sum_{j^{\prime}_{2}=1}^{j_{2}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathcal{I}_{1}\oplus_{0}\mathcal{J}_{1}\in S_{j^{\prime}_{1},j_{1},m}\\
\mathcal{I}_{2}\oplus_{0}\mathcal{J}_{2}\in S_{j^{\prime}_{2},j_{2},m}\end{subarray}}\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}[u_{1},v_{1}]<_{m^{1}_{1}}\dots<_{m^{1}_{j^{\prime}_{1}-1}}[u_{j^{\prime}_{1}},v_{j^{\prime}_{1}}]\in\rho\\
[s_{1},t_{1}]<_{m^{2}_{1}}\dots<_{m^{2}_{j^{\prime}_{2}-1}}[s_{j^{\prime}_{2}},t_{j^{\prime}_{2}}]\in\rho_{n}\end{subarray}}{\sum\ \cdots\ \sum}\bigg[\prod_{l=1}^{j_{1}}\frac{1}{i_{l}!}\bigg]\bigg[\prod_{l=1}^{j_{2}}\frac{1}{e_{l}!}\bigg]

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\sum_{p\in M\!P^{2}_{(2,k^{\prime})}}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\{(\delta_{1},x_{1}),\ \\
(\delta_{2},x_{2})\}\in p\end{subarray}}\mathrm{Cov}\left(X^{(w_{x_{1}})\delta_{1}}_{[u_{l^{1}_{x_{1}}},v_{l^{1}_{x_{1}}}](\mathcal{I}_{1}\oplus_{0}\mathcal{J}_{1})}X^{(w_{x_{1}})(1-\delta_{1})}_{[s_{l^{2}_{x_{1}}},t_{l^{2}_{x_{1}}}](\mathcal{I}_{2}\oplus_{0}\mathcal{J}_{2})},X^{(w_{x_{2}})\delta_{2}}_{[u_{l^{1}_{x_{2}}},v_{l^{1}_{x_{2}}}](\mathcal{I}_{1}\oplus_{0}\mathcal{J}_{1})}X^{(w_{x_{2}})(1-\delta_{2})}_{[s_{l^{2}_{x_{2}}},t_{l^{2}_{x_{2}}}](\mathcal{I}_{2}\oplus_{0}\mathcal{J}_{2})}\right),

by using the fact that, for two collections of mean-zero normal random variables
(Z_{0,1},\ldots,Z_{0,k^{\prime}})
and
(Z_{1,1},\ldots,Z_{1,k^{\prime}})
, we can apply Isserlis’ theorem
(
Isserlis, 1918
)
to show

\displaystyle\mathrm{Cov}

\displaystyle\left(Z_{0,1}\cdots Z_{0,k^{\prime}},Z_{1,1}\cdots Z_{1,k^{\prime}}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[Z_{0,1}\cdots Z_{0,k^{\prime}}Z_{1,1}\cdots Z_{1,k^{\prime}}\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[Z_{0,1}\cdots Z_{0,k^{\prime}}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[Z_{1,1}\cdots Z_{1,k^{\prime}}\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{p\in P^{2}_{(2,k^{\prime})}}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\{(i_{1},i_{2}),\ \ \ \ \\
(j_{1},j_{2})\}\in p\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}\left[Z_{i_{1},i_{2}}Z_{j_{1},j_{2}}\right]-\left(\sum_{q\in P^{2}_{k^{\prime}}}\prod_{\{i,j\}\in q}\mathbb{E}\left[Z_{0,i}Z_{0,j}\right]\right)\left(\sum_{r\in P^{2}_{k^{\prime}}}\prod_{\{i,j\}\in r}\mathbb{E}\left[Z_{1,i}Z_{1,j}\right]\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{p\in M\!P^{2}_{(2,k^{\prime})}}\prod_{\{(i_{1},i_{2}),(j_{1},j_{2})\}\in p}\mathbb{E}\left[Z_{i_{1},i_{2}}Z_{j_{1},j_{2}}\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{p\in M\!P^{2}_{(2,k^{\prime})}}\prod_{\{(i_{1},i_{2}),(j_{1},j_{2})\}\in p}\mathrm{Cov}\left(Z_{i_{1},i_{2}},Z_{j_{1},j_{2}}\right),

where
P^{2}_{(2,k^{\prime})}
denotes the set of all the pairings of
\{0,1\}\times\{1,\ldots,k^{\prime}\}
,
P^{2}_{k^{\prime}}
denotes the set of all the pairings of
\{1,\ldots,k^{\prime}\}
and
M\!P^{2}_{(2,k^{\prime})}
denotes the set of all the pairings of
\{0,1\}\times\{1,\ldots,k^{\prime}\}
that contain at least one “mixed” pair, i.e. for all
p\in M\!P^{2}_{(2,k^{\prime})}
there exist
\{(i_{1},i_{2}),(j_{1},j_{2})\}\in p
such that
i_{1}\neq j_{1}
.

Note that each
[u_{l^{\prime}},v_{l^{\prime}}]
for
l^{\prime}=1,\ldots,j^{\prime}_{1}
appears in at least one covariance term. We proceed by cases:

•

If
[u_{l^{\prime}},v_{l^{\prime}}]
appears in a pair with an interval
[u_{*},v_{*}]
such that
[u_{l^{\prime}-1},v_{l^{\prime}-1}]<[u_{*},v_{*}]<[u_{l^{\prime}+1},v_{l^{\prime}+1}]
, then

|\text{Cov}(X^{(w)}_{[u_{l^{\prime}},v_{l^{\prime}}]},X^{(q)}_{[u_{*},v_{*}]})|\lesssim|v_{l^{\prime}}-u_{l^{\prime}}|,

by applying Assumption (
(A

α

)
) with
\alpha\geq 1/2
and the fact that
\rho
is dyadic (and hence uniform).

•

If
[u_{l^{\prime}},v_{l^{\prime}}]
appears in a pair with an interval
[u_{*},v_{*}]
such that
[u_{*},v_{*}]\leq[u_{l^{\prime}-1},v_{l^{\prime}-1}]
, then we have
|u_{l^{\prime}}-v_{*}|\geq m/2(|v_{*}-u_{*}|+|v_{l^{\prime}}-u_{l^{\prime}}|)
and

|\text{Cov}(X^{(w)}_{[u_{l^{\prime}},v_{l^{\prime}}]},X^{(q)}_{[u_{*},v_{*}]})|\lesssim\theta(|u_{l^{\prime}}-v_{*}|)|v_{*}-u_{*}||v_{l^{\prime}}-u_{l^{\prime}}|\lesssim\theta(|u_{l^{\prime}}-v_{l^{\prime}-1}|)|v_{*}-u_{*}||v_{l^{\prime}}-u_{l^{\prime}}|,

by applying (
(A

θ

)
).

•

Similarly, if
[u_{l^{\prime}},v_{l^{\prime}}]
appears in a pair with an interval
[u_{*},v_{*}]
such that
[u_{l^{\prime}+1},v_{l^{\prime}+1}]\leq[u_{*},v_{*}]
, then we have
|u_{*}-v_{l^{\prime}}|\geq m/2(|v_{l^{\prime}}-u_{l^{\prime}}|+|v_{*}-u_{*}|)
and

|\text{Cov}(X^{(w)}_{[u_{l^{\prime}},v_{l^{\prime}}]},X^{(q)}_{[u_{*},v_{*}]})|\lesssim\theta(|u_{*}-v_{l^{\prime}}|)|v_{l^{\prime}}-u_{l^{\prime}}||v_{*}-u_{*}|\lesssim\theta(|u_{l^{\prime}+1}-v_{l^{\prime}}|)|v_{l^{\prime}}-u_{l^{\prime}}||v_{*}-u_{*}|.

•

If
[u_{l^{\prime}},v_{l^{\prime}}]
appears in a pair with an interval
[s_{*},t_{*}]\in\rho_{n}
, then

|\text{Cov}(X^{(w)}_{[u_{l^{\prime}},v_{l^{\prime}}]},X^{(q)}_{[s_{*},t_{*}]})|\lesssim\theta((n-2)T)|v_{l^{\prime}}-u_{l^{\prime}}||t_{*}-s_{*}|,

by applying (
(A

θ

)
).

A similar reasoning applies to each
[s_{l^{\prime}},t_{l^{\prime}}]
, for
l^{\prime}=1,\ldots,j^{\prime}_{2}
. Noting that at least one pairing is mixed across
\rho
and
\rho_{n}
we can hence bound

\displaystyle|\mathrm{Cov}\left(S^{I}(\mathbb{X}^{\rho,1})_{[0,T]},S^{I}(\mathbb{X}^{\rho,n})_{[0,T]}\right)|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{j_{1},j_{2}=1}^{k^{\prime}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{1},\ldots,i_{j_{1}}\geq 1\\
i_{1}+\cdots+i_{j_{1}}=k^{\prime}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}e_{1},\ldots,e_{j_{2}}\geq 1\\
e_{1}+\cdots+e_{j_{2}}=k^{\prime}\end{subarray}}\sum_{j^{\prime}_{1}=1}^{j_{1}}\sum_{j^{\prime}_{2}=1}^{j_{2}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathcal{I}_{1}\oplus_{0}\mathcal{J}_{1}\in S_{j^{\prime}_{1},j_{1},m}\\
\mathcal{I}_{2}\oplus_{0}\mathcal{J}_{2}\in S_{j^{\prime}_{2},j_{2},m}\end{subarray}}\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}[u_{1},v_{1}]<_{m^{1}_{1}}\dots<_{m^{1}_{j^{\prime}_{1}-1}}[u_{j^{\prime}_{1}},v_{j^{\prime}_{1}}]\in\rho\\
[s_{1},t_{1}]<_{m^{2}_{1}}\dots<_{m^{2}_{j^{\prime}_{2}-1}}[s_{j^{\prime}_{2}},t_{j^{\prime}_{2}}]\in\rho_{n}\end{subarray}}{\sum\ \cdots\ \sum}\bigg[\prod_{l=1}^{j_{1}}\frac{1}{i_{l}!}\bigg]\bigg[\prod_{l=1}^{j_{2}}\frac{1}{e_{l}!}\bigg]

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\sum_{p\in M\!P^{2}_{(2,k^{\prime})}}\theta((n-2)T)|v_{1}-u_{1}|\theta(|u_{2}-v_{1}|)|v_{2}-u_{2}|\cdots\theta(|u_{j^{\prime}_{1}}-v_{j^{\prime}_{1}-1}|)|v_{j^{\prime}_{1}}-u_{j^{\prime}_{1}}|

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\times|t_{1}-s_{1}|\theta(|s_{2}-t_{1}|)|t_{2}-s_{2}|\cdots\theta(|s_{j^{\prime}_{2}}-t_{j^{\prime}_{2}-1}|)|t_{j^{\prime}_{2}}-s_{j^{\prime}_{2}}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{j_{1},j_{2}=1}^{k^{\prime}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{1},\ldots,i_{j_{1}}\geq 1\\
i_{1}+\cdots+i_{j_{1}}=k^{\prime}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}e_{1},\ldots,e_{j_{2}}\geq 1\\
e_{1}+\cdots+e_{j_{2}}=k^{\prime}\end{subarray}}\sum_{j^{\prime}_{1}=1}^{j_{1}}\sum_{j^{\prime}_{2}=1}^{j_{2}}\theta((n-2)T)

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\times\bigg(\sum_{[u_{1},v_{1}]\in\rho}|v_{1}-u_{1}|\bigg)\bigg(\sum_{[u_{2},v_{2}]\in\rho}\theta(|u_{2}|)|v_{2}-u_{2}|\bigg)\cdots\bigg(\sum_{[u_{j^{\prime}_{1}},v_{j^{\prime}_{1}}]\in\rho}\theta(|u_{j^{\prime}_{1}}|)|v_{j^{\prime}_{1}}-u_{j^{\prime}_{1}}|\bigg)

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\times\bigg(\sum_{[s_{1},t_{1}]\in\rho_{n}}|t_{1}-s_{1}|\bigg)\bigg(\sum_{[s_{2},t_{2}]\in\rho_{n}}\theta(|s_{2}|)t_{2}-s_{2}|\bigg)\cdots\bigg(\sum_{[s_{j^{\prime}_{2}},t_{j^{\prime}_{2}}]\in\rho_{n}}\theta(|s_{j^{\prime}_{2}}|)|t_{j^{\prime}_{2}}-s_{j^{\prime}_{2}}|\bigg)

\displaystyle\rightarrow\theta((n-2)T)\left(\sum_{j_{1},j_{2}=1}^{k^{\prime}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{1},\ldots,i_{j_{1}}\geq 1\\
i_{1}+\cdots+i_{j_{1}}=k^{\prime}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}e_{1},\ldots,e_{j_{2}}\geq 1\\
e_{1}+\cdots+e_{j_{2}}=k^{\prime}\end{subarray}}\sum_{j^{\prime}_{1}=1}^{j_{1}}\sum_{j^{\prime}_{2}=1}^{j_{2}}T^{2}\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}\theta(t)\text{d}t\bigg)^{j^{\prime}_{1}+j^{\prime}_{2}-2}\right),\hskip 9.24994pt|\rho|\rightarrow 0.

Combining this bound with (
39
), we can conclude that, for all
n\geq 3
,

\displaystyle|\mathrm{Cov}\left(S^{I}(\mathbb{X}^{1})_{[0,T]},S^{I}(\mathbb{X}^{n})_{[0,T]}\right)|\lesssim\theta((n-2)T)\rightarrow 0,\quad n\rightarrow\infty,

i.e. we have shown (
38
). Hence, we can apply the weak law of large numbers of dependent random variables and the in-fill asymptotics to obtain the desired consistency result.
∎

Appendix C
Variance Reduction via Martingale Correction

In this Section
2.2
we considered a single control obtained by substituting the outermost Stratonovich integral with an Itô integral. In principle, for a word of length
|I|=k\geq 2
, one could consider
2^{k-2}
distinct controls: for any subset
\mathcal{I}\subseteq\{2,\ldots,k-1\}
, one can obtain a control by changing each of the integrals with index in
\mathcal{I}\cup\{k\}
to Itô integrals. One can then apply the controlled linear regression estimator (with only the intercept term as regressor) described in Appendix
G
. This family of controls will likely be highly correlated and hence:

•

the improvements provided by each additional control might be quite marginal compared to the considerable increase in computational cost;

•

the estimator of the (inverse) variance matrix of the controls, needed to make the estimator feasible, might be quite unstable.

Hence, for clarity of exposition and computational ease, throughout the rest of this work we only consider the control variate estimator introduced in Section
2.2
, i.e. for a fixed word
I

\hat{\phi}_{I}^{N,\pi,c}(T):=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\Big(S^{I}(\mathbb{X}^{n,\pi})_{[0,T]}-cS_{c}^{I}(\mathbb{X}^{n,\pi})_{[0,T]}\Big),

(40)

where
I_{-1}:=(i_{1},\ldots,i_{k-1})
and

S_{c}^{I}(\mathbb{X}^{\pi})_{[0,T]}:=\sum_{[u,v]\in\pi}S^{I_{-1}}(\mathbb{X}^{\pi})_{[0,u]}X^{(i_{k})}_{u,v}.

C.1
Martingale Continuity Criterion

If
\mathbb{X}
is a martingale, then, by the Burkholder-Davis-Gundy (BDG) inequality
(
Burkholder et al., 1972
)
, we can write a stronger version of assumptions (
(A

α

)
) in terms of the quadratic variation of
\mathbb{X}
, for all
0\leq s\leq t\leq T

(A
\alpha
.M)

\displaystyle\|\langle\mathbf{X}\rangle_{s,t}\|_{L^{p/2}}\lesssim|t-s|^{2\alpha}
.

Note that for many martingales assumption (
(A

α

.M)
) holds with
\alpha=1/2
and hence, since (
(A

δ

)
) holds trivially, we can usually apply Theorem
2.8
under
(ii)
. Some non-trivial degenerate cases exist: For example, consider a one-dimensional mean-zero Gaussian martingale over
[0,1]
with covariance function
C(s\wedge t)
where
C
is the Cantor function. This process has quadratic variation
\langle X\rangle_{t}=C(t)
and hence – since the Cantor function is Hölder continuous with Hölder exponent
\log_{3}(2)
– assumption (
(A

α

.M)
) is satisfied with
\alpha=\frac{1}{2}\log_{3}2\in(1/4,1/3]
. In this case, one can easily check that (
(A

β

)
) and (
(A

γ

)
) hold by combining the independent increments and martingale property of
\mathbb{X}
.

C.2
Estimating
c_{\pi}^{*}

In Section
2.2
, we considered the following estimator for
c^{*}_{\pi}
:

\hat{c}^{*}_{\pi,1}=\frac{\sum_{n=1}^{N}S^{I}(\mathbb{X}^{n,\pi})_{[0,T]}S_{c}^{I}(\mathbb{X}^{n,\pi})_{[0,T]}}{\sum_{n=1}^{N}S_{c}^{I}(\mathbb{X}^{n,\pi})^{2}_{[0,T]}}.

Alternatively, we can exploit the explicit form of the covariance and variance of the infeasible estimator and approximate

\displaystyle\text{Cov}(S^{I}(\mathbb{X}^{\pi})_{[0,T]},S^{I}_{c}(\mathbb{X}^{\pi})_{[0,T]})

\displaystyle\approx\text{Cov}(S^{I}(\mathbb{X})_{[0,T]},S^{I}_{c}(\mathbb{X})_{[0,T]})

\displaystyle=\sum_{J\in I\shuffle I}\psi_{J}(T)-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{J\in I\shuffle I_{-2}*((i_{k-1},i_{k}))}\psi_{J}(T)

\displaystyle\approx\sum_{J\in I\shuffle I}\hat{\psi}^{N}_{J}(T)-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{J\in I\shuffle I_{-2}*((i_{k-1},i_{k}))}\hat{\psi}^{N}_{J}(T)

\displaystyle\approx\sum_{J\in I\shuffle I}\hat{\psi}^{N,\pi}_{J}(T)-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{J\in I\shuffle I_{-2}*((i_{k-1},i_{k}))}\hat{\psi}^{N,\pi}_{J}(T)

\displaystyle\approx\sum_{J\in I\shuffle I}\hat{\psi}^{N,\pi,\prime}_{J}(T)-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{J\in I\shuffle I_{-2}*((i_{k-1},i_{k}))}\hat{\psi}^{N,\pi,\prime}_{J}(T),

where

\hat{\psi}_{J}^{N}(T):=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}S^{J}((\mathbb{X},\langle\mathbb{X}\rangle)^{n})_{[0,T]},\quad\hat{\psi}_{J}^{N,\pi}(T):=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}S^{J}((\mathbb{X},\langle\mathbb{X}\rangle)^{n,\pi})_{[0,T]},

and

\hat{\psi}_{J}^{N,\pi,\prime}(T):=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}S^{J}((\mathbb{X}^{n,\pi},\langle\hat{\mathbb{X}}\rangle^{n,\pi}))_{[0,T]},

with
\langle\hat{\mathbb{X}}\rangle^{\pi}=\{\langle\hat{\mathbf{X}}\rangle^{\pi}_{t},\ t\in[0,T]\}
defined as

\langle\hat{\mathbf{X}}\rangle^{\pi}_{t}=\sum_{[u^{\prime},v^{\prime}]\in\pi_{[0,u]}}\mathbf{X}_{u^{\prime},v^{\prime}}^{2}+\frac{t-u}{v-u}\mathbf{X}^{2}_{u,v},\quad t\in[u,v].

Similarly, we can approximate

\displaystyle\text{Var}(S^{I}_{c}(\mathbb{X}^{\pi})_{[0,T]})\approx\text{Var}(S_{c}^{I}(\mathbb{X})_{[0,T]})\approx\sum_{J\in I_{-1}\shuffle I_{-1}}\hat{\psi}^{N,\pi}_{J*((i_{k},i_{k}))}(T)\approx\sum_{J\in I_{-1}\shuffle I_{-1}}\hat{\psi}^{N,\pi,\prime}_{J*((i_{k},i_{k}))}(T),

and hence we define the second estimator for
c^{*}_{\pi}
as

\displaystyle\hat{c}^{*}_{\pi,2}

\displaystyle=\frac{\sum_{J\in I\shuffle I}\hat{\psi}^{N,\pi,\prime}_{J}(T)-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{J\in I\shuffle I_{-2}*((i_{k-1},i_{k}))}\hat{\psi}^{N,\pi,\prime}_{J}(T)}{\sum_{J\in I_{-1}\shuffle I_{-1}}\hat{\psi}^{N,\pi,\prime}_{J*((i_{k},i_{k}))}(T)}

\displaystyle=\frac{\sum_{n=1}^{N}\!\Big(\!\sum_{J\in I\shuffle I}\!S^{J}((\mathbb{X}^{n,\pi},\langle\hat{\mathbb{X}}\rangle^{n,\pi}))_{[0,T]}-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{J\in I\shuffle I_{-2}*((i_{k-1},i_{k}))}\!S^{J}((\mathbb{X}^{n,\pi},\langle\hat{\mathbb{X}}\rangle^{n,\pi}))_{[0,T]}\Big)}{\sum_{n=1}^{N}\sum_{J\in I_{-1}\shuffle I_{-1}}S^{J*((i_{k},i_{k}))}((\mathbb{X}^{n,\pi},\langle\hat{\mathbb{X}}\rangle^{n,\pi}))_{[0,T]}}.

Whether estimator
\hat{c}^{*}_{\pi,1}
or estimator
\hat{c}^{*}_{\pi,2}
is more precise, in terms of MSE, depends on the properties of the process
\mathbb{X}
and the expected signature word
I
being estimated by (
40
).

Lemma C.1
.

Let
\mathbb{X}=\{\mathbf{X}_{t},\ t\in[0,T]\}
be a square-integrable martingale satisfying Assumption (
(A

α

.M)
), for some
\alpha\geq 1/2
and
p=4k
where
k=|I|
. Assume that
\pi
is part of a sequence of refining partitions with mesh vanishing fast enough, i.e.

\sum_{n\geq 1}|\pi_{n}|^{2\alpha-1/2}<\infty.

Then the difference between the mean-square errors of the two estimators
\hat{c}^{*}_{\pi,1}
and
\hat{c}^{*}_{\pi,2}
for
c_{\pi}^{*}
is approximately

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[(\hat{c}^{*}_{\pi,2}-c^{*}_{\pi})^{2}]-\mathbb{E}[(\hat{c}^{*}_{\pi,1}-c^{*}_{\pi})^{2}]

\displaystyle\approx\frac{1}{N}\frac{\mu_{Y}}{\mu_{Z}^{3}}\left(\frac{\mu_{Y}}{\mu_{Z}}(\mathbb{E}[Z_{2}^{2}]-\mathbb{E}[Z_{1}^{2}])-2(\mathbb{E}[YZ_{2}]-\mathbb{E}[YZ_{1}])\right),

where

\displaystyle Y=S^{I}(\mathbb{X})_{[0,T]}S_{c}^{I}(\mathbb{X})_{[0,T]},\quad Z_{1}=S_{c}^{I}(\mathbb{X})^{2}_{[0,T]},\quad Z_{2}=\sum_{J\in I_{-1}\shuffle I_{-1}}S^{J*((i_{k},i_{k}))}((\mathbb{X},\langle\mathbb{X}\rangle))_{[0,T]},

and
\mu_{Y}=\mathbb{E}[Y]
,
\mu_{Z}=\mathbb{E}[Z_{1}]=\mathbb{E}[Z_{2}]
.

Proof.

See Appendix
C.3
.
∎

In practical applications, the above expression cannot be evaluated exactly, but we can approximate it by its sample estimate

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[(\hat{c}^{*}_{\pi,2}-c^{*}_{\pi})^{2}]-\mathbb{E}[(\hat{c}^{*}_{\pi,1}-c^{*}_{\pi})^{2}]

\displaystyle\propto\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\left(\frac{\bar{\mu}_{Y}}{\bar{\mu}_{Z}}((Z_{2,n}^{\pi})^{2}-(Z_{1,n}^{\pi})^{2})-\sum_{j=1}^{2}(Y^{\pi}_{j,n}Z^{\pi}_{2,n}-Y^{\pi}_{j,n}Z^{\pi}_{1,n})\right).

where

\displaystyle\bar{\mu}_{Y}=\frac{1}{2N}\left(\sum_{n=1}^{N}Y^{\pi}_{1,n}+\sum_{n=1}^{N}Y^{\pi}_{2,n}\right),\quad\bar{\mu}_{Z}=\frac{1}{2N}\left(\sum_{n=1}^{N}Z^{\pi}_{1,n}+\sum_{n=1}^{N}Z^{\pi}_{2,n}\right),

and
Y_{1,n}^{\pi},Z_{1,n}^{\pi},Y_{2,n}^{\pi},Z_{2,n}^{\pi}
are given in Appendix
C.3
.

Another important discriminant when choosing between estimators
\hat{c}^{*}_{\pi,1}
and
\hat{c}^{*}_{\pi,2}
is usually computational cost. To compute
\hat{c}^{*}_{\pi,1}
it suffices to regress
\{S^{I}(\mathbb{X}^{n,\pi})_{[0,T]},\ n=1,\ldots,N\}
against
\{S_{c}^{I}(\mathbb{X}^{n,\pi})_{[0,T]},\ n=1,\ldots,N\}
. Both samples need to be computed to evaluate the control-variate estimator (
40
) and thus the extra computational cost of
\hat{c}^{*}_{\pi,1}
is just the cost of a simple linear regression with sample size
N
, namely
\mathcal{O}(N)
. On the other hand, to compute
\hat{c}^{*}_{\pi,2}
, one needs to compute all the higher-order expected signature estimates
\hat{\psi}_{J}^{N,\pi,\prime}(T)
with
J\in I\shuffle I
,
J\in I\shuffle I_{-2}*((i_{k-1},i_{k}))
and
J=J^{\prime}*((i_{k},i_{k}))
for
J^{\prime}\in I_{-1}\shuffle I_{-1}
, which has (naive) extra computational cost
\mathcal{O}(|\pi|^{-1}Tk(d^{2k}+(d+1)^{2k-1}))
when parallelizing across the
N
samples. In the in-fill limit,
|\pi|^{-1}T\gg N
and, hence, computing
\hat{c}^{*}_{\pi,2}
is significantly more expensive than computing
\hat{c}^{*}_{\pi,1}
.

C.3
Proof of Lemma
C.1

Sketch of proof.
To compare the two estimators
\hat{c}^{*}_{\pi,1}
and
\hat{c}^{*}_{\pi,2}
we exploit their structure as ratio estimators based on i.i.d. observations of numerator random variables
Y^{\pi}_{1},Y_{2}^{\pi}
and denominator random variables
Z^{\pi}_{1},Z^{\pi}_{2}
respectively. We first show that the two estimators are both (biased) estimators for
c^{*}_{\pi}=c^{*}_{\pi,1}=c^{*}_{\pi,2}
where

c^{*}_{\pi,1}=\frac{\mathbb{E}[Y^{\pi}_{1}]}{\mathbb{E}[Z^{\pi}_{1}]}\hskip 9.24994pt\text{and}\hskip 9.24994ptc^{*}_{\pi,2}=\frac{\mathbb{E}[Y^{\pi}_{2}]}{\mathbb{E}[Z^{\pi}_{2}]}.\vskip-9.24994pt

The first equality is trivial while the second requires several applications of Theorem
2.8
which are detailed in Lemma
C.3
. We then derive a simple formula for the mean squared error of ratio estimators in terms of the means and variances of the numerator and denominator random variables. Taking the limit
|\pi|\downarrow 0
and applying Theorem
2.8
to show the second order statistics of
Y^{\pi}_{1},Y_{2}^{\pi},Z^{\pi}_{1},Z^{\pi}_{2}
converge to those of
Y_{1},Y_{2},Z_{1},Z_{2}
yields the desired result.

Note that both
\hat{c}^{*}_{\pi,1}
and
\hat{c}^{*}_{\pi,2}
are ratio estimators of the form

\hat{c}^{*}_{\pi,j}=\frac{\bar{Y}_{j}^{\pi}}{\bar{Z}_{j}^{\pi}}=\frac{\sum_{n=1}^{N}Y_{j,n}^{\pi}}{\sum_{n=1}^{N}Z_{j,n}^{\pi}},

for random variables
Y_{j,1}^{\pi},\ldots,Y_{j,N}^{\pi}
and
Z_{j,1}^{\pi},\ldots,Z_{j,N}^{\pi}
with
j=1,2
given by i.i.d. copies of

Y_{1}^{\pi}=S^{I}(\mathbb{X}^{\pi})_{[0,T]}S_{c}^{I}(\mathbb{X}^{\pi})_{[0,T]},\quad Z_{1}^{\pi}=S_{c}^{I}(\mathbb{X}^{\pi})^{2}_{[0,T]},

and

\displaystyle Y_{2}^{\pi}=\sum_{J\in I\shuffle I}S^{J}

\displaystyle((\mathbb{X}^{\pi},\langle\hat{\mathbb{X}}\rangle^{\pi}))_{[0,T]}-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{J\in I\shuffle I_{-2}*((i_{k-1},i_{k}))}S^{J}((\mathbb{X}^{\pi},\langle\hat{\mathbb{X}}\rangle^{\pi}))_{[0,T]},

\displaystyle Z_{2}^{\pi}

\displaystyle=\sum_{J\in I_{-1}\shuffle I_{-1}}S^{J*((i_{k},i_{k}))}((\mathbb{X}^{\pi},\langle\hat{\mathbb{X}}\rangle^{\pi}))_{[0,T]}.

By the standard theory of ratio estimators, these are biased estimators for

c^{*}_{\pi,j}=\frac{\mathbb{E}[Y_{j}^{\pi}]}{\mathbb{E}[Z_{j}^{\pi}]},

and applying a first-order Taylor expansion
19
19

19

On the set
|\bar{Z}_{j}^{\pi}-\mathbb{E}[Z_{j}^{\pi}]|<|\mathbb{E}[Z_{j}^{\pi}]|
,

\displaystyle\hat{c}_{\pi,j}

\displaystyle=c^{*}_{\pi,j}\left(1+\frac{\bar{Y}_{j}^{\pi}-\mathbb{E}[Y_{j}^{\pi}]}{\mathbb{E}[Y_{j}^{\pi}]}\right)\left(1+\frac{\bar{Z}_{j}^{\pi}-\mathbb{E}[Z_{j}^{\pi}]}{\mathbb{E}[Z_{j}^{\pi}]}\right)^{-1}

\displaystyle=c^{*}_{\pi,j}\left(1+\frac{\bar{Y}_{j}^{\pi}-\mathbb{E}[Y_{j}^{\pi}]}{\mathbb{E}[Y_{j}^{\pi}]}\right)\left(1-\frac{\bar{Z}_{j}^{\pi}-\mathbb{E}[Z_{j}^{\pi}]}{\mathbb{E}[Z_{j}^{\pi}]}+\mathcal{O}\left(\left(\frac{\bar{Z}_{j}^{\pi}-\mathbb{E}[Z_{j}^{\pi}]}{\mathbb{E}[Z_{j}^{\pi}]}\right)^{2}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=c^{*}_{\pi,j}+\frac{1}{\mathbb{E}[Z_{j}^{\pi}]}(\bar{Y}_{j}^{\pi}-\mathbb{E}[Y_{j}^{\pi}])-\frac{\mathbb{E}[Y_{j}^{\pi}]}{\mathbb{E}[Z_{j}^{\pi}]^{2}}(\bar{Z}_{j}^{\pi}-\mathbb{E}[Z_{j}^{\pi}])+\mathcal{O}\left(\left(\frac{\bar{Y}_{j}^{\pi}-\mathbb{E}[Y_{j}^{\pi}]}{\mathbb{E}[Y_{j}^{\pi}]}\right)\left(\frac{\bar{Z}_{j}^{\pi}-\mathbb{E}[Z_{j}^{\pi}]}{\mathbb{E}[Z_{j}^{\pi}]}\right)\right).

For the approximation to be rigorous one needs to assume the probability of the set
\{\omega:|\bar{Z}_{j}^{\pi}(\omega)-\mathbb{E}[Z_{j}^{\pi}]|\geq|\mathbb{E}[Z_{j}^{\pi}]|\}
approaches 0 faster than the speed at which the MSE conditional on this set explodes as
N\rightarrow\infty
.

we can approximate the mean squared error as

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[(\hat{c}^{*}_{\pi,j}-c^{*}_{\pi,j})^{2}]

\displaystyle\approx\frac{1}{\mathbb{E}[Z_{j}^{\pi}]^{2}}\text{Var}(\bar{Y}_{j}^{\pi})+\frac{\mathbb{E}[Y_{j}^{\pi}]^{2}}{\mathbb{E}[Z_{j}^{\pi}]^{4}}\text{Var}(\bar{Z}_{j}^{\pi})-2\frac{\mathbb{E}[Y_{j}^{\pi}]}{\mathbb{E}[Z_{j}^{\pi}]^{3}}\text{Cov}(\bar{Y}_{j}^{\pi},\bar{Z}_{j}^{\pi})

\displaystyle\approx\frac{1}{N}\left(\frac{1}{\mathbb{E}[Z_{j}^{\pi}]^{2}}\text{Var}(Y_{j}^{\pi})+\frac{\mathbb{E}[Y_{j}^{\pi}]^{2}}{\mathbb{E}[Z_{j}^{\pi}]^{4}}\text{Var}(Z_{j}^{\pi})-2\frac{\mathbb{E}[Y_{j}^{\pi}]}{\mathbb{E}[Z_{j}^{\pi}]^{3}}\text{Cov}(Y_{j}^{\pi},Z_{j}^{\pi})\right).

Note that

c^{*}_{\pi,1}=\frac{\mathbb{E}[S^{I}(\mathbb{X}^{\pi})_{[0,T]}S_{c}^{I}(\mathbb{X}^{\pi})_{[0,T]}]}{\mathbb{E}[S_{c}^{I}(\mathbb{X}^{\pi})^{2}_{[0,T]}]}=\frac{\text{Cov}(S^{I}(\mathbb{X}^{\pi})_{[0,T]},S_{c}^{I}(\mathbb{X}^{\pi})_{[0,T]})}{\text{Var}(S_{c}^{I}(\mathbb{X}^{\pi})^{2}_{[0,T]})}=c^{*}_{\pi},

and, as
|\pi|\rightarrow 0
,

\displaystyle c^{*}_{\pi,2}

\displaystyle=\frac{\sum_{J\in I\shuffle I}\mathbb{E}[S^{J}((\mathbb{X}^{\pi},\langle\hat{\mathbb{X}}\rangle^{\pi}))_{[0,T]}]-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{J\in I\shuffle I_{-2}*((i_{k-1},i_{k}))}\mathbb{E}[S^{J}((\mathbb{X}^{\pi},\langle\hat{\mathbb{X}}\rangle^{\pi}))_{[0,T]}]}{\sum_{J\in I_{-1}\shuffle I_{-1}}\mathbb{E}[S^{J*((i_{k},i_{k}))}((\mathbb{X}^{\pi},\langle\hat{\mathbb{X}}\rangle^{\pi}))_{[0,T]}]}

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eqn:sig_QV_approx_2}}{\approx}\frac{\sum_{J\in I\shuffle I}\mathbb{E}[S^{J}((\mathbb{X},\langle\mathbb{X}\rangle)^{\pi})_{[0,T]}]-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{J\in I\shuffle I_{-2}*((i_{k-1},i_{k}))}\mathbb{E}[S^{J}((\mathbb{X},\langle\mathbb{X}\rangle)^{\pi})_{[0,T]}]}{\sum_{J\in I_{-1}\shuffle I_{-1}}\mathbb{E}[S^{J*((i_{k},i_{k}))}((\mathbb{X},\langle\mathbb{X}\rangle)^{\pi})_{[0,T]}]}

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eqn:sig_QV_approx_1}}{\approx}\frac{\sum_{J\in I\shuffle I}\mathbb{E}[S^{J}((\mathbb{X},\langle\mathbb{X}\rangle))_{[0,T]}]-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{J\in I\shuffle I_{-2}*((i_{k-1},i_{k}))}\mathbb{E}[S^{J}((\mathbb{X},\langle\mathbb{X}\rangle))_{[0,T]}]}{\sum_{J\in I_{-1}\shuffle I_{-1}}\mathbb{E}[S^{J*((i_{k},i_{k}))}((\mathbb{X},\langle\mathbb{X}\rangle))_{[0,T]}]}

\displaystyle=\frac{\text{Cov}(S^{I}(\mathbb{X})_{[0,T]},S_{c}^{I}(\mathbb{X})_{[0,T]})}{\text{Var}(S_{c}^{I}(\mathbb{X})^{2}_{[0,T]})}

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eqn:cov_pi_approx},\eqref{eqn:var_pi_approx}}{\approx}\frac{\text{Cov}(S^{I}(\mathbb{X}^{\pi})_{[0,T]},S_{c}^{I}(\mathbb{X}^{\pi})_{[0,T]})}{\text{Var}(S_{c}^{I}(\mathbb{X}^{\pi})^{2}_{[0,T]})}=c^{*}_{\pi}.

We refer to Lemma
C.3
for the rigorous justification of the approximations (
41
), (
42
), (
43
), (
46
). Moreover, as
|\pi|\rightarrow 0
, by (
41
) and (
42
),

\displaystyle Y_{1}^{\pi}\overset{L^{2}}{\rightarrow}S^{I}(\mathbb{X})_{[0,T]}S_{c}^{I}(\mathbb{X})_{[0,T]}=:Y_{1},\quad Z_{1}^{\pi}\overset{L^{2}}{\rightarrow}S_{c}^{I}(\mathbb{X})^{2}_{[0,T]}=:Z_{1},

and by combining (
43
) and (
46
),

\displaystyle Y_{2}^{\pi}\overset{L^{2}}{\rightarrow}\sum_{J\in I\shuffle I}

\displaystyle S^{J}((\mathbb{X},\langle\mathbb{X}\rangle))_{[0,T]}-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{J\in I\shuffle I_{-2}*((i_{k-1},i_{k}))}S^{J}((\mathbb{X},\langle\mathbb{X}\rangle))_{[0,T]}=:Y_{2},

\displaystyle Z_{2}^{\pi}

\displaystyle\overset{L^{2}}{\rightarrow}\sum_{J\in I_{-1}\shuffle I_{-1}}S^{J*((i_{k},i_{k}))}((\mathbb{X},\langle\mathbb{X}\rangle))_{[0,T]}=:Z_{2}.

Note that
Y_{1}=Y_{2}=:Y
but
Z_{1}\neq Z_{2}
even though
\mathbb{E}[Z_{1}]=\mathbb{E}[Z_{2}]=:\mu_{Z}
. When
|\pi|
is small we can thus approximate the MSEs of
\hat{c}^{*}_{\pi,1}
and
\hat{c}^{*}_{\pi,2}
with respect to
c^{*}_{\pi}
as

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[(\hat{c}^{*}_{\pi,1}-c^{*}_{\pi})^{2}]

\displaystyle\approx\frac{1}{N}\left(\frac{1}{\mu_{Z}^{2}}\sigma^{2}_{Y}+\frac{\mu_{Y}^{2}}{\mu_{Z}^{4}}\text{Var}(Z_{1})-2\frac{\mu_{Y}}{\mu_{Z}^{3}}\text{Cov}(Y,Z_{1})\right),

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[(\hat{c}^{*}_{\pi,2}-c^{*}_{\pi})^{2}]

\displaystyle\approx\frac{1}{N}\left(\frac{1}{\mu_{Z}^{2}}\sigma^{2}_{Y}+\frac{\mu_{Y}^{2}}{\mu_{Z}^{4}}\text{Var}(Z_{2})-2\frac{\mu_{Y}}{\mu_{Z}^{3}}\text{Cov}(Y,Z_{2})\right),

which differ by

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[(\hat{c}^{*}_{\pi,2}-c^{*}_{\pi})^{2}]-\mathbb{E}[(\hat{c}^{*}_{\pi,1}-c^{*}_{\pi})^{2}]

\displaystyle\approx\frac{1}{N}\frac{\mu_{Y}}{\mu_{Z}^{3}}\left(\frac{\mu_{Y}}{\mu_{Z}}(\mathbb{E}[Z_{2}^{2}]-\mathbb{E}[Z_{1}^{2}])-2(\mathbb{E}[YZ_{2}]-\mathbb{E}[YZ_{1}])\right).

Remark C.2
.

Note that we can “mix” the two estimators and form

\hat{c}^{*}_{\pi,2,1}=\frac{\bar{Y}^{\pi}_{2}}{\bar{Z}^{\pi}_{1}}\quad\text{and}\quad\hat{c}^{*}_{\pi,1,2}=\frac{\bar{Y}^{\pi}_{1}}{\bar{Z}^{\pi}_{2}}.

The discussion above ensures that, as
|\pi|\rightarrow 0
,
\hat{c}^{*}_{\pi,2,1}
and
\hat{c}^{*}_{\pi,1,2}
have the same MSEs as
\hat{c}^{*}_{\pi,1,1}=\hat{c}_{\pi,1}
and
\hat{c}^{*}_{\pi,2,2}=\hat{c}^{*}_{\pi,2}
, respectively.

Lemma C.3
.

Assume that
\mathbb{X}
satisfies (
(A

α

.M)
) for some
\alpha\geq 1/2
and
p=4k
where
k=|I|
. Assume that
\pi
is part of a sequence of refining partitions with mesh vanishing fast enough, i.e.

\sum_{n\geq 1}|\pi_{n}|^{2\alpha-1/2}<\infty.

Then the approximations (
41
), (
42
), (
43
), (
46
) hold as
|\pi|\rightarrow 0
.

Proof.

By Appendix
C.1

\mathbb{X}
satisfies (
(A

α

)
) with
\alpha\geq 1/2
and
p=4k
. Note that since
\mathbb{X}
is a martingale (
(A

δ

)
) holds trivially and hence we can set
\epsilon=1/2
.

We can apply Theorem
2.8
to deduce that

S^{I}(\mathbb{X}^{\pi})_{[0,T]}\overset{L^{4}}{\rightarrow}S^{I}(\mathbb{X})_{[0,T]},\quad|\pi|\rightarrow 0.

Moreover,

S_{c}^{I}(\mathbb{X}^{\pi})_{[0,T]}\overset{L^{4}}{\rightarrow}S_{c}^{I}(\mathbb{X})_{[0,T]},\quad|\pi|\rightarrow 0.

since

\displaystyle\|

\displaystyle S_{c}^{I}(\mathbb{X})_{[0,T]}-S_{c}^{I}(\mathbb{X}^{\pi})_{[0,T]}\|_{L^{4}}

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{\leq}\left\|S_{c}^{I}(\mathbb{X})_{[0,T]}-\sum_{[u,v]\in\pi}S^{I_{-1}}(\mathbb{X})_{[0,u]}X^{(i_{k})}_{u,v}\right\|_{L^{4}}+\left\|\sum_{[u,v]\in\pi}(S^{I_{-1}}(\mathbb{X})_{[0,u]}-S^{I_{-1}}(\mathbb{X}^{\pi})_{[0,u]})X^{(i_{k})}_{u,v}\right\|_{L^{4}}

\displaystyle\overset{(ii)}{\leq}\left\|\int_{0}^{T}S^{I_{-1}}(\mathbb{X})_{[0,s]}\text{d}X^{(i_{k})}_{s}-\sum_{[u,v]\in\pi}S^{I_{-1}}(\mathbb{X})_{[0,u]}X^{(i_{k})}_{u,v}\right\|_{L^{4}}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad+2C_{2}\left(\sum_{[u,v]\in\pi}\left\|S^{I_{-1}}(\mathbb{X})_{[0,u]}-S^{I_{-1}}(\mathbb{X}^{\pi})_{[0,u]}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2k/(k-1)}}\|X^{(i_{k})}_{u,v}\|^{2}_{L^{2k}}\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\overset{(iii)}{\lesssim}\left\|\int_{0}^{T}S^{I_{-1}}(\mathbb{X})_{[0,s]}\text{d}X^{(i_{k})}_{s}-\sum_{[u,v]\in\pi}S^{I_{-1}}(\mathbb{X})_{[0,u]}X^{(i_{k})}_{u,v}\right\|_{L^{4}}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad+2C_{2}\sup_{u\in\pi}\left\|S^{I_{-1}}(\mathbb{X})_{[0,u]}-S^{I_{-1}}(\mathbb{X}^{\pi})_{[0,u]}\right\|_{L^{2k/(k-1)}}\left(\sum_{[u,v]\in\pi}|v-u|^{2\alpha}\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\overset{(iv)}{\rightarrow}0,\quad|\pi|\rightarrow 0,

by applying in
(i)
the triangle inequality, in
(ii)
Lemma
B.1
with the natural filtration of
\mathbb{X}
, in
(iii)
Theorem
2.8
to the word
I_{-1}
with
m=2k/(k-1)
and in
(iv)
the definition of the Itô integral and Remark
B.4
.

Under these assumptions, we thus have

S^{I}(\mathbb{X}^{\pi})_{[0,T]}S^{I}_{c}(\mathbb{X}^{\pi})_{[0,T]}\overset{L^{2}}{\rightarrow}S^{I}(\mathbb{X})_{[0,T]}S^{I}_{c}(\mathbb{X})_{[0,T]},\quad|\pi|\rightarrow 0,

(41)

and

S^{I}_{c}(\mathbb{X}^{\pi})^{2}_{[0,T]}\overset{L^{2}}{\rightarrow}S^{I}_{c}(\mathbb{X})^{2}_{[0,T]},\quad|\pi|\rightarrow 0.

(42)

Next, we consider the approximation (
43
). Note that
\mathbb{X}
satisfies (
(A

α

)
) (and trivially (
(A

δ

)
)) with
\alpha\geq 1/2
and
p=4k
while
\langle\mathbb{X}\rangle
satisfies (
(A

α

)
) and (
(A

δ

)
) both with exponent
2\alpha\geq 1
and
p=2k
. By using a slightly more general version
20
20

20

To estimate the expected signature term corresponding to the word
I=(i_{1},\ldots,i_{k})
, it is sufficient to require
(i)
,
(ii)
,
(iii)
, or
(iv)
to be satisfied by
X^{(i_{1})},\ldots,X^{(i_{k})}
with
p_{1},\ldots,p_{k}
such that
p_{1}^{-1}+\dots+p_{k}^{-1}\leq m^{-1}
. In Theorem
2.8
we considered the case where
p_{1}=\dots=p_{k}=p
for clarity.
of Theorem
2.8
applied to the process
(\mathbb{X},\langle\mathbb{X}\rangle)
, for any word
J\in I\shuffle I
or
J\in I\shuffle I_{-2}*((i_{k-1},i_{k}))
or
J=J^{\prime}*((i_{k},i_{k}))
with
J^{\prime}\in I_{-1}\shuffle I_{-1}
we have

S^{J}((\mathbb{X},\langle\mathbb{X}\rangle)^{\pi})_{[0,T]}\overset{L^{2}}{\rightarrow}S^{J}((\mathbb{X},\langle\mathbb{X}\rangle))_{[0,T]},\quad|\pi|\rightarrow 0,

(43)

and hence for fixed
N\geq 1
,

\hat{\psi}^{N,\pi}_{J}(T)\overset{L^{2}}{\rightarrow}\hat{\psi}^{N}_{J}(T),\quad|\pi|\rightarrow 0.

Finally, making approximation (
46
) rigorous is a bit more challenging. Let us start by considering the case where
\langle\mathbb{X}\rangle
only appears in the outermost integral, i.e.
J=(j_{1},\ldots,j_{k^{\prime}})
, where
j_{1},\ldots,j_{k^{\prime}-1}\in\{1,\ldots,d\}
and
j_{k^{\prime}}\in\{(1,1),\ldots,(1,d),\ldots,(d,d)\}
for some
k^{\prime}\in\{1,\ldots,2k-1\}
. Then, for any
\tau\in\pi
,

\displaystyle\Big\|S^{J}(

\displaystyle(\mathbb{X},\langle\mathbb{X}\rangle)^{\pi})_{[0,\tau]}-S^{J}((\mathbb{X}^{\pi},\langle\hat{\mathbb{X}}\rangle^{\pi}))_{[0,\tau]}\Big\|_{L^{4k/(k^{\prime}+1)}}

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{=}\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{k^{\prime}}\frac{1}{i!}\sum_{[u,v]\in\pi_{[0,\tau]}}S^{J_{-i}}(\mathbb{X}^{\pi})_{[0,u]}X_{u,v}^{(j_{k^{\prime}-i+1})}\cdots X_{u,v}^{(j_{k^{\prime}-1})}\left(\langle X\rangle_{u,v}^{(j_{k^{\prime}})}-\langle\hat{X}\rangle_{u,v}^{\pi,(j_{k^{\prime}})}\right)\right\|_{L^{4k/(k^{\prime}+1)}}

\displaystyle\overset{(ii)}{\leq}\sum_{i=1}^{k^{\prime}}\frac{1}{i!}\left\|\sum_{[u,v]\in\pi_{[0,\tau]}}S^{J_{-i}}(\mathbb{X}^{\pi})_{[0,u]}X_{u,v}^{(j_{k^{\prime}-i+1})}\cdots X_{u,v}^{(j_{k^{\prime}-1})}\left(\langle X\rangle_{u,v}^{(j_{k^{\prime}})}-\left(X_{u,v}^{(j_{k^{\prime}})}\right)^{2}\right)\right\|_{L^{4k/(k^{\prime}+1)}}

\displaystyle\overset{(iii)}{\leq}\left(\sum_{[u,v]\in\pi_{[0,\tau]}}\left\|S^{J_{-1}}(\mathbb{X}^{\pi})_{[0,u]}\left(\langle X\rangle_{u,v}^{(j_{k^{\prime}})}-\left(X_{u,v}^{(j_{k^{\prime}})}\right)^{2}\right)\right\|^{2}_{L^{4k/(k^{\prime}+1)}}\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt+\sum_{i=2}^{k^{\prime}}\frac{1}{i!}\sum_{[u,v]\in\pi_{[0,\tau]}}\left\|S^{J_{-i}}(\mathbb{X}^{\pi})_{[0,u]}X_{u,v}^{(j_{k^{\prime}-i+1})}\cdots X_{u,v}^{(j_{k^{\prime}-1})}\left(\langle X\rangle_{u,v}^{(j_{k^{\prime}})}-\left(X_{u,v}^{(j_{k^{\prime}})}\right)^{2}\right)\right\|_{L^{4k/(k^{\prime}+1)}}

\displaystyle\overset{(iii)}{\leq}\left(\sum_{[u,v]\in\pi_{[0,\tau]}}\left\|S^{J_{-1}}(\mathbb{X}^{\pi})_{[0,u]}\left(\langle X\rangle_{u,v}^{(j_{k^{\prime}})}-\left(X_{u,v}^{(j_{k^{\prime}})}\right)^{2}\right)\right\|^{2}_{L^{4k/(k^{\prime}+1)}}\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt+\sum_{i=2}^{k^{\prime}}\frac{1}{i!}\sum_{[u,v]\in\pi_{[0,\tau]}}\left\|S^{J_{-i}}(\mathbb{X}^{\pi})_{[0,u]}X_{u,v}^{(j_{k^{\prime}-i+1})}\cdots X_{u,v}^{(j_{k^{\prime}-1})}\left(\langle X\rangle_{u,v}^{(j_{k^{\prime}})}-\left(X_{u,v}^{(j_{k^{\prime}})}\right)^{2}\right)\right\|_{L^{4k/(k^{\prime}+1)}}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt+\sum_{i=2}^{k^{\prime}}\frac{1}{i!}\ \sup_{u\in\pi_{[0,\tau]}}\left\|S^{J_{-i}}(\mathbb{X}^{\pi})_{[0,u]}\right\|_{L^{4k/(k^{\prime}-i)}}\sum_{[u,v]\in\pi_{[0,\tau]}}\|\mathbf{X}_{u,v}\|^{i-1}_{L^{4k}}\left\|\langle\mathbf{X}\rangle_{u,v}-\mathbf{X}_{u,v}^{\otimes 2}\right\|_{L^{2k}}

\displaystyle\overset{(v)}{\lesssim}|\pi|^{4\alpha-1}\sqrt{\tau}+|\pi|\tau\rightarrow 0,\hskip 9.24994pt|\pi|\rightarrow 0,

by using in

Lemma

, in

\left\|\langle\mathbf{X}\rangle_{u,v}-\mathbf{X}_{u,v}^{\otimes 2}\right\|_{L^{2k}}\leq\left\|\langle\mathbf{X}\rangle_{u,v}\right\|_{L^{2k}}+\left\|\mathbf{X}_{u,v}\right\|^{2}_{L^{4k}}\lesssim|v-u|^{2\alpha},

(44)

by assumption (
(A

.M)
) with

. We have thus shown

\sup_{\tau\in\pi}\Big\|S^{J}((\mathbb{X},\langle\mathbb{X}\rangle)^{\pi})_{[0,\tau]}-S^{J}((\mathbb{X}^{\pi},\langle\hat{\mathbb{X}}\rangle^{\pi}))_{[0,\tau]}\Big\|_{L^{4k/(k^{\prime}+1)}}\lesssim|\pi|.

(45)

This extends to any
J=(j_{1},\ldots,j_{k^{\prime}},\ldots,j_{k^{\prime}+m^{\prime}})
with

for all other

. We proceed inductively on

\displaystyle\Big\|S^{J_{k^{\prime}+m^{\prime}+1}}((\mathbb{X},\langle\mathbb{X}\rangle)^{\pi})_{[0,\tau]}-S^{J_{k^{\prime}+m^{\prime}+1}}((\mathbb{X}^{\pi},\langle\hat{\mathbb{X}}\rangle^{\pi}))_{[0,\tau]}\Big\|_{L^{4k/(k^{\prime}+m^{\prime}+2)}}

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{=}\bigg\|\sum_{i=1}^{m^{\prime}+1}\frac{1}{i!}\sum_{[u,v]\in\pi_{[0,\tau]}}\!\!\!\Big[S^{J_{k^{\prime}+m^{\prime}+1-i}}((\mathbb{X},\langle\mathbb{X}\rangle)^{\pi})_{[0,u]}-S^{J_{k^{\prime}+m^{\prime}+1-i}}((\mathbb{X}^{\pi},\langle\hat{\mathbb{X}}\rangle^{\pi}))_{[0,u]}\Big]X_{u,v}^{(j_{k^{\prime}+m^{\prime}+2-i})}\cdots X_{u,v}^{(j_{k^{\prime}+m^{\prime}+1})}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt+\sum_{i=m^{\prime}+2}^{k^{\prime}+m^{\prime}+1}\frac{1}{i!}\sum_{[u,v]\in\pi_{[0,\tau]}}S^{J_{k^{\prime}+m^{\prime}+1-i}}(\mathbb{X}^{\pi})_{[0,u]}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\times X_{u,v}^{(j_{k^{\prime}+m^{\prime}+2-i})}\cdots\left(\langle X\rangle_{u,v}^{(j_{k^{\prime}})}-\langle\hat{X}\rangle_{u,v}^{\pi,(j_{k^{\prime}})}\right)\cdots X_{u,v}^{(j_{k^{\prime}+m^{\prime}+1})}\bigg\|_{L^{4k/(k^{\prime}+m^{\prime}+2)}}

\displaystyle\overset{(ii)}{\leq}\bigg\|\sum_{[u,v]\in\pi_{[0,\tau]}}\Big[S^{J_{k^{\prime}+m^{\prime}}}((\mathbb{X},\langle\mathbb{X}\rangle)^{\pi})_{[0,u]}-S^{J_{k^{\prime}+m^{\prime}}}((\mathbb{X}^{\pi},\langle\hat{\mathbb{X}}\rangle^{\pi}))_{[0,u]}\Big]X_{u,v}^{(j_{k^{\prime}+m^{\prime}+1})}\bigg\|_{L^{4k/(k^{\prime}+m^{\prime}+2)}}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+\sum_{i=2}^{m^{\prime}+1}\frac{1}{i!}\bigg\|\sum_{[u,v]\in\pi_{[0,\tau]}}\Big[S^{J_{k^{\prime}+m^{\prime}+1-i}}((\mathbb{X},\langle\mathbb{X}\rangle)^{\pi})_{[0,u]}-S^{J_{k^{\prime}+m^{\prime}+1-i}}((\mathbb{X}^{\pi},\langle\hat{\mathbb{X}}\rangle^{\pi}))_{[0,u]}\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\times X_{u,v}^{(j_{k^{\prime}+m^{\prime}+2-i})}\cdots X_{u,v}^{(j_{k^{\prime}+m^{\prime}+1})}\bigg\|_{L^{4k/(k^{\prime}+m^{\prime}+2)}}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+\sum_{i=m^{\prime}+2}^{k^{\prime}+m^{\prime}+1}\frac{1}{i!}\bigg\|\sum_{[u,v]\in\pi_{[0,\tau]}}S^{J_{k^{\prime}+m^{\prime}+1-i}}(\mathbb{X}^{\pi})_{[0,u]}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\times X_{u,v}^{(j_{k^{\prime}+m^{\prime}+2-i})}\cdots\left(\langle X\rangle_{u,v}^{(j_{k^{\prime}})}-\left(X_{u,v}^{(j_{k^{\prime}})}\right)^{2}\right)\cdots X_{u,v}^{(j_{k^{\prime}+m^{\prime}+1})}\bigg\|_{L^{4k/(k^{\prime}+m^{\prime}+2)}}

\displaystyle\overset{(iii)}{\leq}\sup_{u\in\pi_{[0,\tau]}}\Big\|S^{J_{k^{\prime}+m^{\prime}}}((\mathbb{X},\langle\mathbb{X}\rangle)^{\pi})_{[0,u]}-S^{J_{k^{\prime}+m^{\prime}}}((\mathbb{X}^{\pi},\langle\hat{\mathbb{X}}\rangle^{\pi}))_{[0,u]}\Big\|_{L^{4k/(k^{\prime}+m^{\prime}+1)}}\Bigg(\sum_{[u,v]\in\pi_{[0,\tau]}}\|\mathbf{X}_{u,v}\|^{2}_{L^{4k}}\Bigg)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+\sum_{i=2}^{m^{\prime}+1}\frac{1}{i!}\sup_{u\in\pi_{[0,\tau]}}\Big\|S^{J_{k^{\prime}+m^{\prime}+1-i}}((\mathbb{X},\langle\mathbb{X}\rangle)^{\pi})_{[0,u]}-S^{J_{k^{\prime}+m^{\prime}+1-i}}((\mathbb{X}^{\pi},\langle\hat{\mathbb{X}}\rangle^{\pi}))_{[0,u]}\Big\|_{L^{4k/(k^{\prime}+m^{\prime}-i+2)}}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\times\Bigg(\sum_{[u,v]\in\pi_{[0,\tau]}}\|\mathbf{X}_{u,v}\|^{i}_{L^{4k}}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt+\sum_{i=m^{\prime}+2}^{k^{\prime}+m^{\prime}+1}\frac{1}{i!}\sup_{u\in\pi_{[0,\tau]}}\Big\|S^{J_{k^{\prime}+m^{\prime}+1-i}}(\mathbb{X}^{\pi})_{[0,u]}\Big\|_{L^{4k/(k^{\prime}+m^{\prime}+1-i)}}\Bigg(\sum_{[u,v]\in\pi_{[0,\tau]}}\|\mathbf{X}_{u,v}\|^{i-1}_{L^{4k}}\|\langle\mathbf{X}\rangle_{u,v}-\mathbf{X}_{u,v}^{\otimes 2}\|_{L^{2k}}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\overset{(v)}{\lesssim}|\pi|\sqrt{\tau}+|\pi|\tau\rightarrow 0,|\pi|\rightarrow 0,

where in
(i)
we have used Lemma

, in
(ii)
triangle inequality, in
(iii)
Lemma

with the natural filtration of
\mathbb{X}
for the

term, traingle inequality for the

terms and Hölder inequality across all terms, in
(iv)
the inductive hypothesis for
J_{k^{\prime}+m}
with

for the first two sups, Remark
B.4
applied to
\mathbb{X}
and
J_{i}
with
p=4k
,
m=4k/i
and
|J_{i}|=i
for

for the third sup and bounds (

) and (
(A

α

)
) with

and

for the summations over

.

We have thus shown that for any word
J\in I\shuffle I
or
J\in I\shuffle I_{-2}*((i_{k-1},i_{k}))
or
J=J^{\prime}*((i_{k},i_{k}))
for

we have

S^{J}((\mathbb{X},\langle\mathbb{X}\rangle)^{\pi})_{[0,T]}-S^{J}((\mathbb{X}^{\pi},\langle\hat{\mathbb{X}}\rangle^{\pi}))_{[0,T]}\overset{L^{2}}{\rightarrow}0,\quad|\pi|\rightarrow 0,

(46)

and hence for fixed

,

\hat{\psi}^{N,\pi}_{J}(T)-\hat{\psi}^{N,\pi,\prime}_{J}(T)\overset{L^{2}}{\rightarrow}0,\quad|\pi|\rightarrow 0.
